## §0A EXECUTIVE CONSENSUS — WHY THESE ADVANCE

**The book in three lines:**
- What they share: six defensive leaders at or near a new high, 0.48% (KO) to 2.81% (AJG) past their pivots, all graded leaders against the market, five of them in Staples and Financials, and all bought on a break of yesterday's high, never at market.
- Agreement: strongest on PFE, agreed without objection; thinnest on AJG, where the objection is that the ticket buys the high, and on the three group followers (KO, CCEP, BRK-B), each carried over the objection that it trails its group leader.
- What invalidates the group thesis: SPY losing the 750 put wall in a RISK OFF, short-gamma tape, which would send these breakouts back under their pivots together.

**The QA seat's process read (it held one ticket, VRTX, and found the slate does not say exactly what the deciders said):**
- Evidence was read: every ruling cites grid values, and the funnel filtered (151 claims, 302 rulings, 30 names dropped) but moved no stance at tier 1.
- KO: the ticket quotes one decider's early confirmation and leaves his later step to the sidelines out of the dissent list; that move is also missing from the funnel's record of changed stances.
- One trigger word (an hourly close above the prior bar's high) sits on 12 of 14 tickets, watch tickets included, so an alert on a watch ticket fires on the act-now condition; the wait lives only in the plain words, and most of the waiting readings on VLTO, GPN, MA and HOG asked for a pull-in that no ticket text carries.
- Trigger-word reservations count as objections under the trigger rule; counted that way VLTO, GPN and HOG would read contested, not agreed.
- One method's volume bar (1.40 times average) is the only objection on six tickets: CHD, MA, KO, DLTR, CCEP and BRK-B.
- VRTX was held for the room to name one trigger; nobody moved in the hold round, five different triggers stood, and the ticket was dropped on its entry, not on the stock.
- The only market kill filed (index distribution days reaching 4-5 inside 4-5 weeks) has no symbol or level, so it cannot be watched; no decider named a regime, and no cited field has a measured edge.

### PFE — ADVANCE · conviction 4

The only ADVANCE agreed without objection: a leader at the pivot, not a chase, with force building and money flowing in. The case against was timing only (wait for a pull-in to value or a stronger hourly close) and it lost because the entry sits at the pivot. A daily close back under 30.44 is a failed breakout.

### CHD — ADVANCE · conviction 3

It broke out closing near the top of the day on 1.31 times its 20-day volume, AQE rank 34 with force building. Carried over a real objection: the break printed 1.34 times average volume, under the 1.40 bar one method treats as a hard rejection, and it would clear only if the trigger required a 1.40-times session. It has to hold a daily close above 108.93.

### KO — ADVANCE · conviction 3

It closed at 81% of the day's range on 1.26 times its 20-day volume, a leader against the market. Carried over a real objection: KO ranks 218th in Staples while KDP leads at 17th, so this is the follower, and it clears only if KO's own rank moves into the group's top three. The QA seat found the ticket overstates the agreement here: it quotes one decider's early confirmation and leaves out his later step to the sidelines.

### CCEP — ADVANCE · conviction 3

A leader beating the market with its averages in order. Carried over a real objection: it ranks 175th in Staples behind KDP at 17th and broke on 1.18 times average volume, the follower trade one method forbids, and it clears only if its own rank reaches the group's top three. It must not close back under 114.55.

### AJG — ADVANCE · conviction 3

Rising averages 248.67 over 246.32 over 232.17, AQE rank 14, a Financials leader. Carried over a real objection: it is bought at the high, 3.56 daily ranges above its 20-day average with no pullback or contraction, and the objection clears only if the ticket waits for a first pullback toward the 21-day average before the hourly trigger. A daily close back under 271.27 is a failed breakout.

### BRK-B — ADVANCE · conviction 3

A strength close at the break, the one PULLBACK-lane name on the slate. Carried over a real objection: it ranks 245th in Financials behind AJG and GPN on this same slate, and money flow (44.7) and 1.38 times volume have not confirmed; it clears only if its rank reaches the group's top three. It reports on 7 Nov, four sessions out, so decide before the print, not after.

- Dealer gamma: RED light, NEGATIVE; SPY 752.1 vs flip 776.42 (3.23% away), put wall 750.0, call wall 800.0. Assumption, not data: Customers are long calls and long puts, so dealers are long call gamma and short put gamma. This is the standard convention, NOT observed data — exchange feeds publish open interest, never who is on which side.
- Leadership: On both the week and month lists (real leadership): Payments, Homebuilders, Biotech, Travel Leisure, Fintech · Leading from their own highs, not just bouncing: E Commerce, Insurance, REITs, Biotech · Strong numbers but still well off their highs (a bounce, not leadership yet): Agri Commodities, Infra Construction, Cloud Computing, Gaming Streaming
- What would change the stance (thresholds already met are dropped by the file): Stocks above their 40-day line clears 50.0 (now 42.7); 5-day up/down 4% count clears 1.0 (now 1.0); 5-day up/down 4% count falls under 0.7 (now 1.0). The monthly 350-risers line is a whole-market number and is not reachable on this panel.

In the room only VLTO and MA carry a graded setup (delayed episodic pivot, FAILED); none of the room's names is TRIGGERED or READY.
- Doctrine, quoted from the handbook and not an Aegis rule: "Breadth is against the tape — build the watchlist, do not push new entries."

## Read with care (declared gaps)

- Data: AQE export 06:54:12 SGT carrying the Mon 2 Nov close, 350 rows, 2 held (live), 0 data-quality flags; Crown file DEGRADED (its cross-checks disagree on the volatility gap; oldest source 30 Oct, 4 days behind).
- Book and stops: 2 Nov close journal cross-checked against a live read-only Tiger pull at 07:30 SGT, 0 disagreements; Tiger's COIN, CRCL, CRDO, KO, MARA, MCD and NVDA are outside the Aegis book per the journal.
- Ledgers: the phase4 ledger is from the 29 Oct run (the 2 Nov run pushed none), so repeat counts come from that base; 366 repeat-watch rows have no verdict-ledger match; no packet stamp on main (md5 recorded at prepare).
- INBOX-CLEAN degraded: the first courier stalled and the second was killed by the container restart; the third found 13 threads, trashed 12, left 6 GitHub notifications (the failed AQE daily run faf2789 and five failed voice-packet syncs), and one AQE thread refused the trash call and could not be verified.
- Closes for the scorecard: 3,296 points checked by code, 0 mismatches; ACA ends 22 Oct and KVUE has no 2 Nov row.
- Fundamentals: pulled after ranking for the 49-name room (49/49), hand-transcribed by two couriers and spot-checked exact against fresh FMP calls on MA, PG, V and KMPR.
- MTF tool defect, patched at runtime: the tool stored only a name-level summary, so the first vote packet read 'MTF not served' for every name; per-frame summaries were added with the tool's own function (unpatched file kept, fix on the engineering branch, not pushed); side effect: the packet reads 'W W:'.
- Hourly bars are derived: FMP 15-minute bars rolled by code into :30-anchored hourly bars (checked exact against FMP hourly on FE, volume included); five series were matched to tickers by best price fit (FA, CHD, DLTR, ETSY, KMPR) and MP was refetched after a filename collision.
- Vote packet 106 KB, over the 70 KB soft cap, not trimmed; every decider filed three parts, all validated and merged.
- Container restart during the cross review killed two reviewers and two inbox couriers mid-work; both reviewers were respawned once from scratch and validated; the run directory survived and was checkpointed.
- Funnel measurement: 151 claims routed, 302 rulings (79 confirm, 190 refute, 33 could not see); 14 names survived tier 1, 30 were dropped, 5 had no backing at the blind vote; stances changed on a peer's evidence: 0 of 302.
- Spend: the conductor's meter (4 bytes per token) ended above the 2,500,000 ceiling and the PM authorised continuing; actual subagent tokens run higher than the meter (about 417k for gather couriers and 1.09M for MTF and fundamentals couriers, killed attempts unmetered).
- QA finding: the KO ticket quotes one decider as confirming and leaves his later step to the sidelines out of the dissent list, so the slate does not say exactly what the deciders said.
- QA finding: the same trigger word (an hourly close above the prior bar's high) is the trigger on 12 of 14 tickets, watch tickets included, so an alert on a watch ticket fires on the act-now condition; the wait lives only in the plain words.
- QA finding: one decider's seven trigger-word reservations count as objections under the trigger rule; counted that way VLTO, GPN and HOG would read contested, not agreed.
- QA finding: one decider's volume test (1.40 times average) is the only objection on six tickets.
- Market kill: only one decider filed a market condition (index distribution days reaching 4-5 inside 4-5 weeks); it carries no symbol or level, so the alert loop cannot watch it.
- Render note: PFE's one trigger is a daily buy stop one tick over 31.04, while the card's trigger row prints the alert loop's hourly close at the same level; GPN's one trigger is yesterday's high 100.37, while its condition row prints the 99.63 closing-high line.
- Breadth figures in the weather are on AQE's own panel (US-listed, over $2B), not the whole market; Valen's whole-market thresholds are not reachable on it.
